2026-07-09 | Blueprint | Claude Code (Sonnet 5) | master (docs only) | Investigated a separate, genuinely reproducible bug: `loginController.js` (and, newly discovered here, ~20 other controller files) store large JSON blobs plus a JWT as browser cookies via `$cookies.putObject`/`$cookies.put`; cumulative cookie size trips Tomcat's "Request header is too large" error on every request after login, confirmed live against a fresh session on `his-local-tomcat`. Ran 3 parallel research agents: (1) enumerated all 34 distinct cookie keys app-wide (12 previously known + 22 newly found, 20 live + 2 dead/commented-out), with write-site file:line and traced data shapes/size estimates; (2) enumerated every read site for all 12 originally-known keys, and corrected a prior session's claim that `cookieObject.authToken` was read via "a centralized ~1031-call interceptor" — there is no `$httpProvider.interceptors` registered anywhere; `cookieObject` is actually read 1,196 times across 1,022 files (not just for the Authorization header), and `cookieObjectAppConfig` 247 times across 231 files; (3) searched the entire Java backend (8,254 files) for direct `HttpServletRequest.getCookies()`/`Cookie` usage and found the critical fact that changes this migration's risk profile: `SessionHandlerInterceptor.java` (registered globally for `/api/**`) and 15 redundant controllers DO read `cookieObject`/`cookieObjectAppConfig` directly off the raw Cookie header (feeding `SessionService`, used by 343 files) — but confirmed via exhaustive scan that none of the other 32 cookie keys (including `authToken`, which `JwtTokenFilter` reads only from the `Authorization` header) have any Java-side dependency at all. Wrote the full inventory, read-site lists, Java-dependency table, and a phased migration recommendation (Phase A: 22 fully client-only keys → sessionStorage, zero backend risk, do first; Phase B: split `cookieObject`/`cookieObjectAppConfig` into a slim Java-required cookie + sessionStorage for the rest, needs its own design pass and touches `SessionHandlerInterceptor`) to `docs/COOKIE_BLOAT_MIGRATION_PLAN.md`, including flagged open questions (`GetStoreListUsingsessionService.java`'s untraced dependency on `cookieObject`, a `SessionService` singleton-scope thread-safety concern, and a suspected but unverified nursing-station cookie duplication). 2026-07-10 | Maker | Claude Code (Sonnet 5) | fix/appointment-token-number-collision | Fixed a genuine date-scoping bug in `AppointmentSchedulingDaoImpl.getTokenNumber(String currentDate, Integer unitId)`: the method's SQL query (`SELECT max(token_number) ... WHERE unit_id=:unitId AND to_char(appointment_date,'YYYY-MM-DD')=:date`) ignored its own `currentDate` parameter and always bound `:date` to `TimeZoneComponent.getDateByzone(unitId)` (server "today"), so token-number scoping was keyed to the current server date instead of the appointment's actual target date -- causing token_number collisions for bookings not made "today" relative to server time. Root cause confirmed live via real Postgres/Tomcat logs on `his-local-tomcat`/local dev Postgres, not just code inspection. ## 2026-07-15 (later same day) — investigate/razorpay-client-trusted-amount (docs-only, no application code, no billing math touched)
Role: Blueprint (docs only, explicit owner instruction). Per owner direction, wrote `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_RazorPayClientTrustedAmount.md` covering the two endpoints flagged out-of-scope in the earlier Razorpay 10x-undercharge document (`getOrderIdForRazorPayPayment`, `getOrderIdUnitSubscriptionPay`), treated with the same priority. Investigation went beyond the two endpoints' own bodies (already known: both put a client-supplied `razorPayBillAmount` into a real Razorpay order with no server-side fee computation) and traced what consumes that output, surfacing a materially more severe combined finding: (1) the amount later recorded as "paid" in the actual bill (`confirmAppointment` -> `saveBillFromKioskFutureDate` -> `settleBill` -> `collectPayment`) is sourced from `m_razorpay_order_log_details.amount`, populated by a *different* client-supplied field (`reqDto.getAmount()`) than the one actually sent to Razorpay (`razorPayBillAmount`) -- confirmed via `RazorPayDaoImpl.java`'s `logRazorPayOrder`/`getAppointmentDataByRazorPayOrderId`, never cross-checked against each other; (2) for the unit-subscription flow, the amount recorded as paid comes directly from an Angular scope variable in the browser (`selfRegistrationController.js`'s Razorpay `handler` callback and `settleTranaction()`), whose only "validation" compares that value against itself, and whose source contains a commented-out call (`// $scope.verifyRazorPayPayment(response);`) suggesting server-side verification was intended and never completed; (3) full-codebase search found zero Razorpay payment-signature verification anywhere -- `razorpay_signature` is read into a local variable in 3 places (`confirmAppointment`, `confirmAppointment1`, `confirmPayment`) and never referenced again; (4) read (not runtime-tested) `JwtTokenFilter.java` in full and found these endpoints -- all mapped at root level with a single path segment -- appear to satisfy its anonymous-access fallback (`splitUrl.length <= 2`), with `getOrderIdUnitSubscriptionPay` additionally hardcoded into its exemption list twice by name; flagged this as high-confidence but explicitly unproven without a live runtime test, which was not performed. Also updated `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_RazorPay10xUndercharge.md` to point §6 at the new document (closing that open item) and to record that historical-reconciliation and deployment-verification remain on hold per explicit owner instruction (owner will supply the reconciliation answer separately; no DB query was run). No fix was proposed or implemented for any of the above -- decision document only, per instruction to hold billing-math/code changes. | `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_RazorPayClientTrustedAmount.md` (new), `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_RazorPay10xUndercharge.md`, this log | N -- no `try/catch`, database column, or billing math changed; no manifest BR row added, consistent with existing convention that `DECISION_NEEDED_*` docs are not themselves manifest rows until a fix is decided

## 2026-07-15 (later still) — PHASE 1 Razorpay/auth: pre-approval verification, two confirmed findings, fix draft revised (docs-only)
Role: Blueprint (docs only). Owner withheld approval of the drafted low-risk fix (`docs/REVIEW_DRAFT_razorpay-auth-fix-NOT-APPLIED.md` §1+§2) pending two specific answers. **Answer 1 (token-before-gate question): traced both `bookAppointmentController.js` and `selfRegistrationController.js` end to end, confirmed NEITHER anonymous booking/self-registration flow ever writes an `authToken` into the cookie at any point before calling the Razorpay order-creation endpoints -- `GenericService.js` (the shared HTTP wrapper) only ever reads `cookieObject.authToken`, never writes it.** Corrected an earlier misreading of my own: the `generateToken`/`tokenNumber` fields in `selfRegistrationController.js` are a hospital queue ticket number (displayed in a "your token is #42" modal), not a JWT -- unrelated to authentication. Conclusion: §2 (the drafted `JwtTokenFilter.java` patch requiring a valid JWT) would break real, legitimate anonymous patient bookings if applied as drafted -- confirmed unsafe, not just a residual risk. Corrected direction recorded in the doc: rely on Razorpay's own signature (§1's approach, which doesn't require a session) as the trust boundary for this surface instead of a JWT requirement, since payment-gateway confirmation callbacks are inherently anonymous by design. **Answer 2 (saveAndPay question): read `BillingMasterController.java`'s `saveBillFromKiosk`/`saveBillFromKioskFutureDate` (`/kiosk/saveAndPay`, `/kiosk/saveAndPay/future`) in full -- confirmed the same client-trusted-amount issue, arguably worse.** Both take `EncounterMasterDto` (including its full client-supplied `paymentReceiptDetailsDtosList`) directly from the request body and pass it straight to `collectPayment(...)` with zero Razorpay involvement of any kind; the only gateway check present is CCAvenue-specific (transaction-reuse only, not Razorpay, not amount-verifying). This means the originally-drafted §1 fix alone does not close the subscription/kiosk flow's exposure, since a request could bypass order-creation/confirmation entirely and POST straight to `saveAndPay`. Flagged as needing its own fix (same signature-verification pattern as §1, but architecturally more involved since `saveAndPay` is a general multi-payment-mode endpoint, not Razorpay-specific) -- not drafted this session, pending owner direction. Updated `docs/REVIEW_DRAFT_razorpay-auth-fix-NOT-APPLIED.md` in place with both findings; §3/§4 (SecurityConfig wiring) untouched, held per owner instruction for separate review. **No file under `legacy/` modified; nothing applied, committed to legacy code, or deployed.** | `docs/REVIEW_DRAFT_razorpay-auth-fix-NOT-APPLIED.md`, this log | N -- no `try/catch`, database column, or billing math changed; investigation and doc-revision only

4. Tolerance for wave-1 characterization tests confirmed as proposed — strict, zero wiggle room (exact equality), not RULES.md's $0.01 circuit-breaker tolerance (that's reserved for wave-2+ real-data comparisons).
5. The "which known quirks to preserve vs. fix" triage (RISK-1/2/4/5, ONE-OFF-2) is **explicitly not being decided now** — owner asked to keep logging inconsistencies as they surface during test-writing and triage them together later, rather than one at a time as found.

**Work this session:** wrote the first wave-1 characterization test, `legacy/Global/src/test/java/com/param/global/common/BillingCommonMethodsCharacterizationTest.java`, targeting `BillingCommonMethods.updatePreAuthAndServiceCapAmountForSecondContract` (7 call sites in `ContractConsumptionServiceImpl.java`, the #1-ranked entry point). Global had zero test infrastructure of its own before this (only the inherited parent `junit:junit:3.8.1`, compile-scope, per `AUDIT_TestCoverage.md`) — added `junit-jupiter:5.10.2`, `mockito-core`/`mockito-junit-jupiter:5.11.0`, `assertj-core:3.25.3`, all `scope=test` (does not ship in the module's production artifact; Global's own compiler target is untouched at Java 17, this session is not part of the Java-upgrade track). 10 test cases across 5 groups (no-adjustment baseline, co-share adjustment, pre-auth ceiling, the `updateCap`-already-set guard clause, and an explicit "known gap" group) — all mock `IContractConsumptionDao` via package-private field assignment (no Spring context needed), so each test runs in well under a second with zero database dependency. Verified via `mvn -pl Global test`: BUILD SUCCESS, 10/10 pass, confirmed from a clean `target/` wipe (not just an incremental rebuild).

**Genuine finding while writing the tests, not while looking for bugs:** traced all 7 call sites of `updatePreAuthAndServiceCapAmountForSecondContract` and confirmed the method's co-share/pre-auth-ceiling adjustment to `totalSelfPayable`/`totalCreditPayable` never reaches the caller — the parameters are `Double` (pass-by-value in Java, so reassigning them inside the method is invisible to the caller) and the returned `ContractConsumptionDto` only ever gets `setContractId` called on it, never `setSelfPayable`/`setCreaditPayable`. Meanwhile the *database* writes (`updatePreAuthAmount`/`updateCapAmount`/`updateServiceCapAmount`) correctly receive the adjusted amount. Net effect: the insurance/pre-auth/cap ledger and the patient's own bill can disagree on second-round contracts with a co-pay percentage or a hit pre-auth ceiling. Per owner instruction, logged clearly as `BR-PENDING-copay-preauth-split-return-value-gap` in `docs/BUSINESS_RULE_MANIFEST.md`/`BR_INDEX.md` and **not fixed** — the 2 tests in the `ReturnValueGap` group exist specifically to pin this exact current behavior so any future change to it (fix or accidental) shows up as a failing assertion.

**Boundaries held:** no file under `legacy/Billing`, `legacy/Entity`, or any of the 9 originally-blocked modules touched. Only Global's `pom.xml` (test-scope additions only) and one new test file changed. Worked on a dedicated branch; `main`/`origin/main` untouched. ## 2026-07-15 (later still) — Shadow-test wave 1 continues: Procurement's tax computation (entry point #2, second half) — 3-way divergence found and logged

Role: Maker, same branch (`shadow-test/wave1-copay-split`), no subagents. Not pushed.

**Target:** Procurement's `GoodReceiptNoteService.approveGoodReceiptNote` (~line 1752-1776) — the module previously flagged as a near-duplicate of Inventory's tax computation, per the prior session-log entry's own "not yet covered" note.

**Genuine finding, discovered by reading both sources side by side while writing the test, not by looking for a bug:** the two "duplicate" tax computations are not actually the same. Three confirmed divergences, all pinned by passing/failing test assertions rather than inferred from reading alone: (1) Procurement nets a discount off the amount before taxing (`commTaxAmount = totalAmount - totalDiscount`); Inventory taxes the raw amount with no discount subtraction, despite `ReturnableGatepassDetail` having its own populated `totalDiscount` field (confirmed via grep: set at 12 other call sites in the same file) that the tax block simply never reads; (2) Procurement has no null-guard on the detail's tax reference and calls `detail.getTax().getId()` directly outside the try/catch that wraps the rest of the loop — a detail with no tax assigned throws an uncaught `NullPointerException` that aborts the *entire* GRN approval, not just that one line item (confirmed via a test asserting the exception is thrown); Inventory falls back to a default of 0 in the same situation; (3) Procurement's detail loop has no equivalent of Inventory's `isDeleted` skip — every detail is processed unconditionally.

**Work this session:** wrote `legacy/Procurement/src/test/java/com/param/procurement/purchase/service/goodReceiptNote/GoodReceiptNoteServiceCharacterizationTest.java`, 8 test cases (the core discount-net formula, null-discount-treated-as-zero, unclamped-negative-result, multiple tax components, the null-tax-reference crash, the no-deleted-guard confirmation, and two edge cases). Added the same test toolkit as Inventory/Global to Procurement's `pom.xml`, bumping its existing `mockito-core:1.10.19` to `5.11.0` in place (test scope only). Verified via `mvn -pl Procurement test` (full module), from a clean `target/` wipe: BUILD SUCCESS, 8/8 pass, all on the first real run (no compile-error iteration needed this time).

**Logged per owner instruction** (Decision 5, standing): `BR-PENDING-grn-vs-returnable-gatepass-tax-divergence` in `docs/BUSINESS_RULE_MANIFEST.md`/`BR_INDEX.md`, covering all 3 divergences. **Not fixed** — neither `ReturnableGatepassService.java` nor `GoodReceiptNoteService.java` was modified; the characterization tests exist so any future change to either formula (deliberate or accidental) shows up as a failing assertion rather than a silent drift.

**Owner reviewed the draft and approved logging + committing.** | `legacy/Procurement/pom.xml`, `legacy/Procurement/src/test/java/com/param/procurement/purchase/service/goodReceiptNote/GoodReceiptNoteServiceCharacterizationTest.java` (new), `docs/BUSINESS_RULE_MANIFEST.md`, `docs/BR_INDEX.md`, this log | N — no local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed; the known-issue log entry documents 3 found divergences without fixing any of them, matching the `BR-PENDING-copay-preauth-split-return-value-gap` precedent

## 2026-07-15 (later still) — Shadow-test wave 1, entry point #3: collectPayment, unguarded-null-check found and logged

Role: Maker, same branch (`shadow-test/wave1-copay-split`), no subagents. Owner directed continuation through entry points #3-5 without stopping between them, unless a genuine business-logic ambiguity (not a technical one) came up. None did for #3. Not pushed.

**Re-verified first:** confirmed via `git log` that `BillingMasterServiceImpl.java` is unchanged since baseline (matches the original audit citation). Read the full `collectPayment` method (~480 lines, 1877-2357) before writing anything.

**Scope decision, made and disclosed rather than silently assumed:** unlike entry points #1/#2, `collectPayment` is not a small, single-purpose method -- beyond the core payment/due-amount arithmetic, it also orchestrates 3 SMS-notification branches, an external MyLife/Global-Scheduling HTTP sync (raw `RestTemplate` calls plus 2 unconditional raw Hibernate SQL queries via `sessionFactory`), patient loyalty-card consumption, appointment/encounter status updates, and 5 payment-mode-specific integration side effects (Mpesa, Pesapal, RazorPay, CCAvenue, POS machine). Scoped this session's characterization tests to the core due-amount arithmetic (RISK-1's specific citation: `dueAmount`/`totalSelfDueAmount`/`totalCreditDueAmount`) and the validation gates guarding it, choosing fixture values (`isInterimOrFinalBill=0`, `appointmentId=null`, `patientCouponId=null`, empty payment-receipt-details list, `patientId=null`) that route execution around the SMS/sync/loyalty/appointment branches rather than mocking all ~15 additional dependencies those would require. Logged this scope boundary explicitly in the test's own javadoc and in the manifest entry below, per the "no silent caps" principle -- not silently treating partial coverage as complete.

**Genuine finding, discovered while building the fixture, not by hunting for a bug:** of the 3 SMS-trigger branches, 2 null-guard `isInterimOrFinalBill` before comparing it to a literal; the 3rd does not, and throws a real `NullPointerException` (auto-unboxing a null `Integer`) when `isInterimOrFinalBill` is null and `visitTypeId` isn't OP -- wrapped into a generic `BillingException` by the method's outer catch-all, so the caller can't distinguish it from any other unexpected failure. Confirmed via a passing test that asserts the exact exception type.

Added the full JUnit 5 + Mockito + AssertJ toolkit to Billing's `pom.xml` (Billing had no test framework at all before this). Hit and fixed 2 real test-authoring bugs of my own during this pass, not production bugs: (1) initially stubbed `Response.getStatus()` to return `"SUCCESS"` (uppercase) when the actual `ICommonConstants.SUCCESS` constant is lowercase `"success"` -- case-sensitive `.equals()` silently failed the whole success-path branch and surfaced as a downstream `NullPointerException` on an unconditionally-dereferenced `savePaymentres` variable several hundred lines later, not as an obvious stubbing-mismatch error; (2) `savePaymentReciept`'s real signature takes a boxed `Double` for `totalDueAmount`, not a primitive `double` -- `anyDouble()` doesn't match a boxed-`Double` argument position, needed `any(Double.class)` instead. ## 2026-07-15 (later still) — Shadow-test wave 1, entry point #4: consumeDeposit, 3 findings pinned and logged

Role: Maker, same branch (`shadow-test/wave1-copay-split`), no subagents. Continuing directly per owner instruction (no ambiguity requiring a judgment call came up). Not pushed.

**Re-verified first:** confirmed via `git log` that `PaymentDepositConsumptionServiceImpl.java` is unchanged since baseline. Read the full method (~80 lines, much more contained than entry point #3's collectPayment) before writing anything -- this is also the exact method `docs/AUDIT_BillingRisk.md` RISK-3 already flags for the lost-update/absolute-`SET` deposit-balance pattern; scoped this session's tests to what `consumeDeposit` itself computes and passes to its collaborators (`IDepositMasterService.updateDepositMaster`/`updateDepositDetails`), not to those collaborators' own DAO write semantics (a separate layer, RISK-3's own concern).

**Three genuine findings, all discovered by reading closely while building fixtures, not by hunting for bugs -- all pinned by passing test assertions, not just described:**
1. **Most concrete:** the inner detail-row loop's `if(consumedAmount>0)` guard wraps the *entire* `while` loop, not each iteration -- once entered, every remaining detail row in the list gets touched, even after the running `consumedAmount` has reached 0. Each of those rows still gets a real `updateDepositDetails` database write (consumedAmount forced to 0, balance numerically unchanged) -- a real, redundant write per unconsumed row, not merely a theoretical inefficiency. Initially mis-modeled this in a first draft test (assumed the loop would simply stop); the test failure ("wanted 1 time but was 2 times") is what surfaced the real behavior, corrected before logging.
2. `billId`/`billPayeeDetailsId` null-checks are inverted from the usual "fill in if missing" idiom -- they only overwrite when the field is *already* non-null, so a deposit arriving with `billId == null` never gets the request's `billingMasterId` attached.
3. A read-then-write-back of `consumedAmount` (read the DTO's own field, immediately write the same value back) has zero observable effect -- likely dead code from an earlier refactor, not a live bug.

**Also hit and fixed one test-authoring lesson of my own:** an initial attempt to independently re-derive an expected rounded/subtracted double value via a parallel `Math.round(...)`-then-subtract expression in the test produced a different last-bit result (`233.33999999999997`) than the method's actual output (`233.34`), despite looking like the same arithmetic. Per Decision 4's own principle, switched to pinning the literal observed value directly rather than re-deriving it through a separate expression -- a concrete illustration of why that principle matters, not just an abstract rule.

**Work this session:** wrote `legacy/Billing/src/test/java/com/param/billing/global/transaction/service/PaymentDepositConsumptionServiceImplCharacterizationTest.java`, 8 test cases across 4 groups (single-detail-row consumption incl. the rounding pin, multi-row spanning consumption incl. the post-exhaustion no-op writes, the inverted null-check, the read-write-back no-op). Verified via `mvn -pl Billing test` (full module, both entry points #3 and #4 together): BUILD SUCCESS, 21/21 pass, confirmed from a clean `target/` wipe.

**Logged per owner instruction** (Decision 5, standing): `BR-PENDING-consumedeposit-findings` in `docs/BUSINESS_RULE_MANIFEST.md`/`BR_INDEX.md`, covering all 3 findings in one entry (same batching convention as the Procurement/Inventory tax-divergence entry). **Not fixed** — `PaymentDepositConsumptionServiceImpl.java` was not modified. | `legacy/Billing/src/test/java/com/param/billing/global/transaction/service/PaymentDepositConsumptionServiceImplCharacterizationTest.java` (new), `docs/BUSINESS_RULE_MANIFEST.md`, `docs/BR_INDEX.md`, this log | N — no local `try/catch` touched, no database column changed; known-issue log entry only, no fix applied

**One mistake made and corrected, disclosed in full rather than silently fixed**: the HIS-WAR OpenRewrite invocation needed `-am` (also-make) to resolve the WAR aggregator's full dependency set under offline mode. Maven's `-am` flag runs the specified goal on *every* project in the resolved reactor, not just the `-pl`-selected one -- this was not anticipated, and it caused the `UpgradeToJava21` recipe to also run directly against `main`'s working-tree checkout of ADT, Abdm, OTIMS, Inventory, Procurement, and Pharmacy (all still on Java 17 in `main` at that point, since their real fixes lived only on their own branches), leaving 166 files of stray, uncommitted, unreviewed changes sitting on `main` -- including one genuinely new change to the shared root `legacy/pom.xml` (`<maven.compiler.release>21</maven.compiler.release>` added, `maven-war-plugin` bumped) that had never been reviewed or tested anywhere. Caught via `git status` immediately after returning to `main`. Verified byte-for-byte (via `diff` against each already-committed branch) that every module-specific stray change was a pure, redundant duplicate of already-committed, already-tested branch work -- none of it was unique. Rather than discarding outright, ran `git stash push -u` (reversible, not destructive) to clear `main`'s working tree back to matching `origin/main` exactly, preserving the stray diff recoverably in the stash in case this assessment turns out to be wrong. Implements Tier 0 and Tier 1 of the proposal exactly as scoped, per owner instruction. New, additive infrastructure only -- no existing calculation, validation, or behavior changed anywhere.

**Tier 0** (the flag mechanism): new `m_circuit_breaker_state` table (`docs/br099_circuit_breaker_state.sql`, single row, system-wide, seeded `NORMAL`; applied to the local `his_local` dev database and confirmed), new `CircuitBreakerState` entity (extends `CommonEntity`, deliberately not `BaseEntity` -- no org/unit scoping for a system-wide flag), DAO/service/controller following this codebase's existing conventions exactly (`UnitApplicationBillingConfigurationDaoImpl`/`ReferalDetailsController` used as templates), exposing `GET/POST /global/circuitbreaker/state,flip,reset`.

**Tier 1**: (a) `.github/workflows/reactor-tests.yml` runs the full reactor suite on every push/PR to `main` -- automates condition 3 (shadow test failure rate), using the 141-test suite this session already built; (b) `ErrorCorrelationUtil` (already called at all 11 existing correlation-ID sites) now tracks a rolling 24h occurrence count; new `CircuitBreakerMonitorScheduler` in HIS-WAR polls it every 60 seconds (matching RULES.md's stated interval literally) and flips the Tier 0 flag `NORMAL`→`LEGACY` past a threshold of 5, never auto-resetting -- automates condition 7; (c) `scripts/check_trycatch_removal.py`, a diff-based heuristic PR check (same "candidate for manual verification" spirit as this project's other heuristic scripts) wired into the same workflow on `pull_request` events -- automates condition 6. Conditions 1, 2, 4, 5 explicitly not implemented (Tier 2/3, deferred, matching the proposal doc).

One compile error caught and fixed during this work, not a test failure: `Session.get()` in this codebase's Hibernate version returns `Object`, not a generic type, so the initial DAO code needed an explicit cast in 3 places -- caught immediately by the reactor build itself, fixed, rebuilt clean.

## 2026-07-17 (later still) — Frontend work begins: Phase 0 Angular screen inventory + dual risk classification, docs only

Role: Blueprint/Architect (read-only inventory, no application code touched), committed directly to `main` per the docs/governance-file carve-out, not pushed.

First frontend-focused session; scope explicitly widened from "billing system" to "complete HIS" with a two-axis risk model (clinical-safety in addition to billing) before this work started. Confirmed the real AngularJS app lives at `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp` (not `legacy/AngularSpring`, which has no `views/`). Parsed the single `$stateProvider` config (`resources/assets/js/config/app.js`, 22,471 lines) with a small Python balanced-paren scanner rather than reading it by hand -- found 1,098 live `.state()` definitions across 51 modules (117 more were commented-out/dead and excluded). Cross-referenced every state's `templateUrl` against the real `views/` tree and every `controller` name against actual `.controller(...)` registrations in the 1,205 controller files (658 of 660 distinct names resolved). Tagged each screen for clinical-safety and billing risk using a keyword+module heuristic; caught and fixed a real bug in that heuristic mid-session (naive substring matching was flagging almost every "...Master"-titled screen as clinical via a stray `'er '` match, and "...List" screens via `'lis'` matching inside "List") before trusting the output -- switched to word-boundary regex with an explicit stem/exact-word distinction per keyword. Final tally: 836/1,098 screens (76%) touch clinical safety or billing in some way; only 259 (23%) look administrative on this first pass -- a materially bigger high-risk surface than a billing-only lens would have found, which is the whole reason the scope correction mattered. Wrote `docs/ANGULAR_PHASE0_SCREEN_INVENTORY.md` (full per-screen table, generated programmatically rather than typed by hand, to keep 1,098 rows internally consistent) with known gaps (9 unresolved controllers, 3 multi-view states, 13 dead template paths, 372 screens sharing generic controllers) called out explicitly rather than guessed at. No code changed; no rule in `RULES.md` touched. Committed to `main`, held for owner review before Phase 1 (audit-extraction/golden-trace tooling) begins.

## 2026-07-17 (later still) — Investigated 2 flagged security-relevant findings (false alarm, own tooling bug); Phase 1 tooling built and validated

Role: Blueprint/Architect for the investigation (read-only, no code changes without sign-off, per owner instruction); Maker for the Phase 1 tooling (new files only, docs/tooling carve-out, no application code touched). Committed directly to `main`, not pushed.

**Investigation**, as owner-requested: checked whether the 2 unresolved-controller findings flagged as security-relevant in Phase 0 (`roleManagement` -> `roleManagementMasterController`, `userAuthentication` -> `userAuthenticationMasterController`) were live broken routes. **Both are false alarms.** Both controllers exist, are correctly registered on `angular.module('myApp')`, and are loaded via ordinary `<script>` tags in `index.html` -- the routes are not broken. Root cause was in this session's own Phase 0 tooling, not the app: the controller-resolution scan only searched `resources/assets/js/controller/`, missing the sibling `resources/assets/accessControl/controller/` directory both files actually live in. While root-causing that, found and fixed a second tooling bug (a regex that excluded `.` from controller names, missing `otBillingCompletionDetails`'s dotted controller name) and one more false-positive from the directory-scope bug (`noteMaster`). Corrected controller resolution end to end (9 unresolved -> 5, believed genuine), which surfaced two unrelated real findings logged in the inventory doc's "known gaps" (not owner-actionable bugs, just transparency): `accessControlController` is registered in two different files (silent last-load-wins ambiguity), and 4 dead git-merge-conflict-artifact files sit in the Nursing/pharmacy controller path. **No application code was touched at any point in this investigation** -- only `docs/ANGULAR_PHASE0_SCREEN_INVENTORY.md` was corrected.

**Phase 1, greenlit after the above was reported:** built `tools/angular-audit/` (productionized the ad hoc Phase 0 scripts into a one-command, re-runnable pipeline -- `run_audit.py` -- with the controller-resolution fixes baked in from the start; verified it reproduces the corrected inventory doc exactly, zero data-row diffs) and `tools/golden-trace/` (runtime characterization tooling, the owner's named Phase 1 priority: trace `common-controller.js`'s/`MasterController`'s internal branching, since 353 screens depend on it and static analysis alone can't resolve what any one of them does). `tracer.js` hooks AngularJS's own module-loading mechanism via a two-level property interceptor (`window.angular`, then `.module` landing on that object) to install a `$controller` decorator, `$http` interceptor, `$rootScope` decorator, and state-transition listeners the moment the app defines `myApp` -- pure runtime injection via Playwright's `addInitScript`, no file under `legacy/` read or written by the mechanism. `capture.js` serves the webapp statically, seeds a synthetic permission set past the app's access-control resolve guards, and drives navigation via `$state.go(name)`. Validated end-to-end against 27 real `MasterController`-shared screens spanning 9 modules: 25/27 confirmed the controller active with the correct state name at the moment of instantiation, empirically. The 2 that failed to route independently corroborated two of Phase 0's static "dead templateUrl" findings via a live `$stateChangeError` -- same problem, found two different ways, a genuine cross-validation of both tools. Documented plainly in both READMEs: no live Java backend/DB in this environment, so captured `$http` calls are real attempts (method/URL/order/state all accurate) but never get a real response -- full end-to-end traces need this pointed at a live deployment. No rebuilt screens were built; no rule in `RULES.md` touched. Committed to `main`, held for owner review.

## 2026-07-17 (later still) — Resolved accessControlController duplicate; Phase 2 pilot batch approved and audited (5 screens)

Role: Blueprint/Architect throughout (read-only audit, no application code touched, no rebuild started). Committed directly to `main`, not pushed.

**Housekeeping before the pilot:** owner asked which of the two `accessControlController` registrations (flagged as an unresolved ambiguity in the last session) actually wins at runtime. Checked `index.html`'s script-tag order: `resources/assets/accessControl/controller/accessControlController.js` (a 60-line stub) loads first, `resources/assets/js/controller/access-control/access-control.js` (373 lines, the full implementation) loads second -- AngularJS's `.controller()` silently overwrites same-named registrations, so the second one wins. Baked this fact into `generate_inventory.py` (not just a doc edit) so it survives future re-runs.

**Phase 2 pilot batch proposed and approved:** 5 screens spanning low-risk/billing-only/clinical-only/cross-screen-coupling/MasterController-shared, deliberately not all one controller type -- `MISReports`, `insuranceMaster`, `inputOutputCharts`, `organizationMaster` (doubling as the low-risk and coupling pick), `homecareMedicationApproval` (the MasterController + both-risk stress test). Reasoning for each pick given and approved before any audit work started, per standing rule.

**Audit step (step 1 of the pilot process) completed for all 5, written to `docs/PHASE2_PILOT_AUDIT.md`.** Read every view and controller file directly rather than trusting the Phase 0 static table; three things worth carrying forward:

1. Quantified a Phase 0 caveat rather than leaving it a guess: static form-field counts overcount by ~30-40% on this sample (commented-out fields not stripped before counting).
2. Found and flagged, not fixed: `organizationMaster` is missing a `$state` injection its login-redirect guard depends on (`'use strict'` means this throws `ReferenceError` instead of redirecting when a session cookie is missing) -- confirmed isolated to this one file by comparing against `insuranceMaster`'s identical, correctly-injected guard. Left for owner decision, same standing rule as every backend finding.
3. The owner's explicit ask for extra care on `homecareMedicationApproval` (given it's the hardest pilot case) paid off: read all 463 lines of `common-controller.js` and found `MasterController` has **no state-based branching anywhere** -- corrects Phase 0's own stated hypothesis ("likely branches internally on the current state/URL"), now updated in the regenerated inventory doc. Bigger finding underneath that: `homecareMedicationApproval` itself turned out to be a fully static UI mockup with zero live data bindings (hardcoded table rows, no `ng-repeat`, no `ng-model` anywhere) -- confirmed both by reading the view and by its golden trace (captured per explicit instruction; exactly one HTTP call, the template fetch itself, tagged with this state, nothing else). Flagged as a decision point rather than assumed: rebuild the mockup as-is, or swap this pilot slot for a real MasterController+both-risk screen.

Golden-trace capture and any rebuild work deliberately not started for the other 4 screens -- held per explicit instruction until the owner reviews audit quality, since this sets the pattern for every subsequent screen in the project. No rule in `RULES.md` touched.

## 2026-07-17 (later still) — Golden-trace capture completed on all 5 final pilot screens; found and fixed a real bug in the capture tooling itself, plus one more real app finding

Role: Blueprint/Architect (docs) and Maker for the tooling fix (`tools/golden-trace/capture.js`, project tooling, docs/tooling carve-out applies, not application code -- committed directly to `main`). Not pushed.

Owner approved `addItemPOCreation` as the replacement pilot screen; proceeded with golden-trace capture on all 5. First pass was wrong in a way worth catching before reporting it as an app finding: 4 of 5 traces showed only a template fetch, no real API calls -- including `insuranceMaster`, which the source-reading audit had already established makes 6 real calls on load. Didn't report the mismatch as an app fact; traced it instead. Root cause, confirmed with a cookie-write/timing diagnostic (full JS stack captured, not guessed): `loginController.js` (lines 44-48) unconditionally clears every browser cookie on every instantiation, and every capture transits through the `login` state first as part of normal app bootstrap -- so any cookie seeded before page load (how `capture.js` originally worked) gets wiped before the target screen is ever reached. Confirmed this is deliberate, sensible app behavior (clean session on the login screen), not a bug -- the bug was in the harness. Fixed by seeding `cookieObject` after bootstrap completes instead of before; found and fixed a second instance of the same class of gap the same empirical way (`MISReportsController`/`inputOutputChartsController` also gate on a second cookie, `cookieObjectAppConfig`, redirecting to a real separate state, `applicationConfiguration`, when it's missing).

Re-captured all 5. **4/5 now show real, multi-call traces matching the section-by-section audit exactly** (`insuranceMaster`: 6 calls, `organizationMaster`: 8, `inputOutputCharts`: 3, `MISReports`: 4). **`addItemPOCreation` still shows only its template fetch** -- investigated rather than assumed still-broken-harness: its view uses `controller as vm` syntax, but its state definition routes it through `MasterController`, which has no `controllerAs` and defines no `vm` -- every `vm.model.*` binding (98 `ng-model`, 113 `ng-click`) silently resolves against `undefined`, no error, just inert. A real, apparently-complete controller for this exact pattern exists (`addNewPOController.js`, `self.model = {...}`, real pagination/GRN/file-upload logic) but is never referenced by any state in `app.js` -- confirmed by grep, zero matches. Orphaned from its route. This is a third, distinct failure mode this pilot has now found (not a static mockup, not an isolated missing-DI crash, but a real controller disconnected from its screen), logged as a decision point in `docs/PHASE2_PILOT_AUDIT.md` section 7 with three honest options and no recommendation forced -- not resolved unilaterally. The captured trace for `addItemPOCreation` was left as-is (accurately reflects current inert behavior) rather than "fixed" by capturing the orphaned controller instead.

Also corrected `build_branch_map.py`'s boilerplate output text, which still asserted the "MasterController branches on state" hypothesis the prior session's audit had already disproven by reading `common-controller.js` in full.

No rule in `RULES.md` touched. No rebuild work started -- audit and golden-trace capture (steps 1-2 of the pilot process) are now complete for all 5 final screens; rebuild (step 3) has not begun.

## 2026-07-17 (later still) — addItemPOCreation resolved (real controller, wrong route); found Phase 0 missed 468 live states; pilot slot swapped a third time to a confirmed-working screen

Role: Blueprint/Architect (docs only, no application code touched this entry). Committed directly to `main`, not pushed.

Owner asked for a completeness read on `addNewPOController.js` before deciding `addItemPOCreation`'s fate, plus a pilot slot swap from the 58 confirmed-real `MasterController` screens, plus logging the disconnection as a pending finding rather than resolving it inside the pilot. Read the controller in full (4,833 lines): no TODO/stub markers, a complete real `SavePurchaseOrder` flow with a genuine save call and correct field mapping across two PO types -- not abandoned code. Then found why it seemed disconnected: the file self-registers its *own* state, `addItemPO` (not `addItemPOCreation`), correctly wired with `controllerAs: 'vm'` this time, live (script tag confirmed loaded) -- and Phase 0's inventory never knew this state existed, because it's registered outside `app.js` entirely.

**That single discovery generalized into the biggest finding of this session.** Checked how many other controller files do the same self-registration (`$stateProvider.state(...)` inside their own `.config()` block, outside `app.js`): 465 files. Extracted every state they define and cross-referenced against the known 1,098 -- 475 distinct names, zero overlap (purely additive), 468 confirmed live via `index.html`'s actual uncommented script tags (the other 7 are commented-out or the already-known dead merge-artifact files). **The app has roughly 1,566 real screens, not 1,098 -- every Phase 0 number produced so far (module breakdown, risk tiers, the 353/32%-`MasterController` figure) was computed against ~70% of the app.** Spot-checked three of the new ones at random (`GRN`, `IPDBilling`, `SurgicalSiteInfection`) -- all real, substantial, business-purpose screens, not test code. Logged as its own entry (`docs/ANGULAR_PENDING_FINDINGS.md` entry 3) rather than silently absorbed or acted on -- re-scanning and re-classifying the full set is on the order of redoing Phase 0 itself, a decision for the owner on priority, not something to fold into this pilot.

Logged `addItemPOCreation`'s disconnection as its own pending finding (entry 2, three options given, none chosen), per instruction. Swapped the pilot slot a third time -- this time verified empirically via golden-trace capture *before* picking, rather than trusting the static-binding list again (that list is exactly what produced `addItemPOCreation` in the first place): tried `addNewStaffRegistration` and `fundApproval` first (both showed only a template fetch, no real activity), landed on `billingGroupMaster` (4 real API calls captured, matches source). Its working mechanism turned out to be a fourth distinct wiring pattern this pilot has now found: the view embeds its own `ng-controller="BillingGroupMasterController"` directive directly in the HTML, layering real logic on top of the generic `MasterController` as a nested scope -- confirmed both in the trace (both controllers appear) and in source. Final 5-screen batch (`MISReports`, `insuranceMaster`, `inputOutputCharts`, `organizationMaster`, `billingGroupMaster`) golden-trace captured and cross-checked against the source-reading audit for all 5.

No rule in `RULES.md` touched; no application code changed. Rebuild (step 3) deliberately not started this entry -- the 468-live-states discovery is significant enough to report and let the owner weigh in on before committing further effort to Phase 2 specifically, rather than proceeding to rebuild on the letter of the prior instruction while a ~30%-larger-than-known chunk of the app sits completely unassessed.

## 2026-07-18 — Rebuilt all 5 Phase 2 pilot screens in modern Angular; wrote the requested Phase 0 re-scope estimate

Role: Maker for the rebuild (dedicated branch `rebuild/phase2-pilot-batch`, application code, Maker-Checker applies, not merged, not pushed); Blueprint/Architect for the estimate doc (docs/tooling carve-out, committed directly to `main`).

Owner's instruction was explicit: proceed to rebuild (step 3) on the approved 5-screen batch now, since the pilot's job (proving the process) is done and is separate from the missed-states question; produce a short effort/timeline summary of the 468-states discovery in parallel, not the full re-scan; owner retains the call on when the full corrected Phase 0 re-scan happens relative to this rebuild and Phase 3.

**Rebuild:** stood up a new Angular 22 workspace (`new/`, standalone components, signals, new control-flow syntax, `npx @angular/cli@latest`, no NgModules) and rebuilt `billingGroupMaster`, `insuranceMaster`, `organizationMaster`, `inputOutputCharts`, and `MISReports` -- all 5. Verified building together with zero errors (`npx ng build`, 5 lazy chunks generated). Several parity decisions carried as class-level doc comments rather than silent choices: `insuranceMaster` keeps the legacy dual-validation redundancy and the real save/update response-shape asymmetry (`docs/ANGULAR_PENDING_FINDINGS.md` entry 1); `organizationMaster` uses the already-approved `$state`-injection fix rather than reproducing the known bug, and leaves unreachable legacy sub-panels (licence-details table, BI/OTIMS tabs) unrebuilt as out-of-scope; `inputOutputCharts` stubs PDF generation (no backend to target in this pilot) and uses a minimal inline patient search in place of shared partials Phase 2 never independently audited; `MISReports` (the most architecturally complex screen -- a dynamic report-parameter engine driven by backend `controlName` metadata) collapses ~19 distinct `controlName` values to the smaller set of UI patterns the legacy view actually renders, and leaves `Servicesearch`/`DiagnosiSearch`/`VenderSearch` typeaheads rendering-but-unwired since this pilot never audited those endpoints in depth. Committed to `rebuild/phase2-pilot-batch` (55 files, `new/` workspace only, `node_modules`/`dist` correctly gitignored), held for review, not merged, not pushed.

**One scope decision from this rebuild not yet put to the owner for sign-off, flagging now rather than letting it pass silently:** live ngUpgrade hybrid-mode bootstrap wiring into the actual served `legacy/HIS-WAR` app was deliberately not attempted this pass. These are standalone, working Angular components structured so they *can* be embedded via ngUpgrade later (Phase 7, per the existing plan), but that live wiring itself is not done -- none of the 5 screens are reachable from the running legacy app today. Worth an explicit go/no-go before Phase 3 assumes otherwise.

**Re-audit against golden traces and domain-expert review (the final two steps of the stated per-screen process) have not started for any of the 5** -- this entry covers rebuild only.

**Phase 0 re-scope estimate**, as requested: `docs/ANGULAR_PHASE0_RESCOPE_ESTIMATE.md`. Roughly 4-5 person-days to extend `extract_states.py` beyond `app.js`, re-run the existing pipeline unmodified against the corrected ~1,566-state count, and reconcile every previously-cited aggregate figure (module splits, risk-tier percentages, the 353/32%-`MasterController` share) -- most of that time is reconciliation and review, not new engineering. Confirmed explicitly, since it was asked: the risk-tier classification logic and the rest of the audit tooling need no changes, only `extract_states.py`'s file-walking scope. Flagged that any phase-duration estimate already extrapolated from the 1,098 figure is undercounting remaining work by roughly the same ~43% the state count was short by, and that the 468 missed states concentrate in the same high-risk areas (Pharmacy Inventory, Master data, Procurement) Phase 0 already flagged -- more volume of known risk, not a new category of it. Did not perform the re-scan itself; sequencing left to the owner as instructed. Committed directly to `main`, not pushed.

No rule in `RULES.md` touched.

## 2026-07-18 (later still) — Second Phase 2 pilot batch: audited + rebuilt 5 controllerAs:'vm' screens, 2 of the original 5 picks swapped after turning out non-functional, golden-trace re-audit caught a systematic URL bug

Role: Maker for the rebuild (dedicated branch `rebuild/phase2-pilot-batch2-vm-pattern`, branched from `rebuild/phase2-pilot-batch` so `new/` workspace conventions carried over, application code, Maker-Checker applies, not merged, not pushed); Blueprint/Architect for the audit doc and pending-findings entries (docs/tooling, committed directly to `main`).

Owner approved running a second pilot batch (proposed in the prior session, not yet acted on) targeting the `controllerAs:'vm'` self-registered screen population -- 91% of the 468 screens the corrected Phase 0 re-scan found, a wiring pattern the first pilot's final 5 screens barely touched. Proposed 5 screens, deliberately mixed risk tier: `GRN` (Procurement, billing-only), `SurgicalSiteInfection` (Nursing, clinical-only), `OTBillingCompletion` (OT/Theatre, both), `assigningPickerDispenser` (Pharmacy Inventory, billing-only), `assetCategoryMaster` (Admin/Asset Management, low-risk).

**Audit found 2 of the 5 originally-picked screens were not viable, same discipline as the first pilot's `homecareMedicationApproval`/`addItemPOCreation` swaps, not silently substituted:**
- `SurgicalSiteInfection` is a non-functional copy-pasted mockup -- zero `ng-model` bindings anywhere in its SSI-specific markup, every click handler references functions that exist only in sibling controllers, the form's own `name` attribute is literally `medicationErrorReporting`. Swapped for `periodicityService` (Nursing, clinical-only), verified real before committing (127 `self.*` vs. 107 `$scope.*` assignments, 25 confirmed `vm.*` view bindings).
- `OTBillingCompletion` is a bigger version of the `addItemPOCreation` failure class: its view is an ~90%-copy-pasted clone of a *completely different* screen's template (`details-OT-billing-completion.html`, belonging to a separate, correctly-wired state backed by a genuine 7,000+-line controller), sharing almost no real bindings with the small controller actually wired to this route. Swapped for `implantMaster` (Procurement - Inventory, both clinical + billing), independently re-verified (43 of 45 view bindings resolve correctly, proportionate 528-line view / 836-line controller, unlike `OTBillingCompletion`'s 1,172-line view against a 129-line controller).

**Real bugs found in the 3 non-swapped screens plus the 2 replacements, all logged, none fixed:** a dead `PopulateReasonDropdown()` reference in `GRN`; a *third* independent occurrence of the missing-`$state`-DI crash bug already fixed once for `organizationMaster` (found in both `implantMaster` and `assetCategoryMaster`); a real data-loss bug in `implantMaster`'s speciality-picker (a speciality added mid-edit-session saves with `id: undefined`, silently dropped); a real crash bug in `assigningPickerDispenser` (`sessionStorage.selectedTopStore` dereferenced before its `null` check) that was independently confirmed live by the golden-trace capture tool itself, not just read from source -- the capture's own call count matched exactly what the crash would produce; plus three smaller bugs in `assetCategoryMaster` (search silently scoped to the current page only, a status-toggle error-revert bug, a copy-paste validation-message bug). All 7 new findings + the swap decisions logged in `docs/ANGULAR_PENDING_FINDINGS.md` entries 5-11, including a methodology caveat (entry 11): `controllerAs:'vm'` being declared in a state's config doesn't reliably mean the view uses `vm.*` bindings -- found 3 Nursing screens where it's dead configuration, meaning the "91%" figure from entry 3 may overstate how many of the 468 screens actually need this pattern's distinct handling.

**Rebuild:** all 5 final screens built in `new/`, verified building together with zero errors. **Re-audit against golden traces caught a real, systematic bug this rebuild itself introduced**: every URL built from a `CONSTANTS.GLOBAL.*`/`CONSTANTS.PHARMACY.*`/`CONSTANTS.PROCUREMENT.*`/`CONSTANTS.MASTER.UNIT.FIXED_ASSET.*` constant resolves through a base variable that itself starts with `/api/` (`globalApi`, `pharmacyApi`, `procurementApi` -- note plural, `unitsApi`) -- the first draft of every one of this batch's 5 services dropped that prefix. Caught by cross-checking golden-trace's actually-fired URLs against the rebuild's service code directly (not by re-reading the audit writeups), fixed across all 5 services, confirmed clean rebuild afterward. Full detail, including which legacy bugs were deliberately reproduced for parity vs. which are architecturally impossible to reproduce (the `$state` crashes, since `SessionService` never throws) vs. which are structurally self-correcting the same way `insuranceMaster`'s ledger backfill already was (entry 4): `docs/PHASE2_PILOT_BATCH2_AUDIT.md`.

**This branch's history includes two merges from `main`** (`fc6d77b`, `4d40514`) to pick up docs-only commits that landed on `main` after `rebuild/phase2-pilot-batch` was originally cut -- done deliberately before editing `ANGULAR_PENDING_FINDINGS.md`, to avoid silently reverting entry 4 or the Phase 0 re-scan resolution that had already been committed there.

Domain-expert review (still pending from the first pilot, per `docs/PHASE2_DOMAIN_REVIEW_GUIDE.md`) and the 2 blocked circuit-breaker branch pushes remain the owner's, untouched this session. No rule in `RULES.md` touched.

## 2026-07-18 (later still) — Merged the 3 approved $state-DI fixes into main; set up the batch-1 domain-review environment (legacy Tomcat + rebuild ng serve); found and fixed 4 local-dev-DB drift gaps blocking legacy login

Role: Maker. Two parts, both owner-directed, neither pushed.

**Part 1 — merges.** Owner approved all 3 `$state`-injection crash fixes (`organizationController.js`, `assetCategoryMasterController.js`, `implantMasterController.js` -- same bug pattern, confirmed via direct file reads to be 3 independently-authored controllers, not one shared fix). Merged both fix branches into local `main` (2 clean merge commits, no conflicts). `mvn -pl HIS-WAR -am compile -DskipTests`: BUILD SUCCESS, all 18 modules. `main` now 6 commits ahead of `origin/main`, not pushed.

**Part 2 — domain-review environment.** Confirmed the local Postgres (`his_local`, Homebrew) and Tomcat 9 (already running since earlier the same day) serve the real app at `http://localhost:8080/`. Checked out `rebuild/phase2-pilot-batch` (batch 1 only, deliberately not batch 2) and ran `ng serve --proxy-config proxy.conf.json` (created from `proxy.conf.example.json`, targeting `http://localhost:8080`) at `http://localhost:4200/`. Verified both live via real requests (not just commands run) -- legacy root, rebuild root + 2 specific routes, and confirmed the proxy genuinely reaches the legacy backend (`/api/global/status` returns `401` through the proxy, not a connection error). Materialized `docs/PHASE2_DOMAIN_REVIEW_GUIDE.md`/`RESULTS.md` into the rebuild-branch working tree (untracked copies, since those docs live on `main` which predates that branch and switching would have deleted files out from under the running dev server) so the owner could read/fill them without a branch switch.

**Owner then reported legacy login broken** (empty Organization/Unit dropdowns, can't log in) and asked for root-cause investigation, not just a restart. Confirmed Tomcat's deployed `jdbc.properties` matches source exactly (no config drift) -- the problem was DB content. Traced every blocking error live via `curl` against the real endpoints + Tomcat's `catalina.out` stack traces, cross-checked against each table's actual `@Entity` mapping rather than guessed DDL, and found 4 local-dev-DB gaps, all consistent with genuine drift since the earlier Razorpay-test session (10 days prior in-story) rather than anything this session touched: a missing column (`m_organization_master.is_duo_authentication_required`), a missing table (`m_different_types_screen_image`), and a missing table (`m_unit_subscription_details`) that a prior session (BR-039) had explicitly found pre-existing and left untouched -- now gone, confirming the drift theory rather than a new regression. Chasing the dependency chain further (rather than stopping at the first fix and letting the owner hit the next wall), also found `m_unit_subscription_config`/`t_unit_subscription_details`/the `pgcrypto` extension missing -- the same 3-piece chain BR-038/BR-039 had already built and fully verified once before -- and recreated all of it using the exact same proven-good seed values that session already validated, rather than deriving new placeholders. Verified via direct `curl`, not assumed: both blocking endpoints (`organizationMasterList`, `getUnitByCredentials`, the latter with the documented `admin`/`12345` credential) now return real success responses with the `DEMO` org/unit data. Separately investigated the reported "username prefilled with 1049" -- read `loginController.js` in full, confirmed no prefill logic exists anywhere; almost certainly browser autofill, not an app bug, reported as such rather than chased further. Logged as `BR-local-dev-db-drift-legacy-login-blocked` in `docs/BUSINESS_RULE_MANIFEST.md` + `docs/BR_INDEX.md`, same classification as BR-038/BR-039 (Neither, local-dev-only, no sign-off required). Used a git worktree (`/tmp/his-main-logging`) to commit this documentation to `main` without disturbing the `new/` files the running `ng serve` process depends on.

Domain-expert review itself has still not been attempted by any AI session -- remains entirely the owner's, on their own schedule, per standing instruction. The 2 blocked circuit-breaker branch pushes also remain untouched. No rule in `RULES.md` touched (all DB changes were additive-only per RULES.md item 1; no `try/catch` touched).

## 2026-07-18 (later still) — AI-driven interaction-level sweep begins across all 10 Phase 2 pilot screens (batch 1 + batch 2), using new browser-automation capability; billingGroupMaster toggle bug root-caused (2 stacked issues), a systemic zero-access-control finding surfaced, 2 local-dev-DB gaps found and closed (owner-approved mid-session)

Role: Blueprint/Architect for investigation and findings-logging (no application code fixed, per standing instruction — findings only); Maker for the DB-only fixes below (owner-approved exception to this session's original frontend-only scope). Docs committed directly to `main` (governance-file carve-out); no application code changed this entry.

**Context:** picked up after a context clear, continuing the owner's manual side-by-side domain review of batch 1. The owner had found `billingGroupMaster`'s Status toggle looked disabled on the rebuild and asked for root-cause investigation (not a fix) plus a systematic interaction-level sweep of all 10 pilot screens using the newly available browser-automation tooling — the first time any AI session has driven real clicks/saves/toggles rather than just initial page-load golden-traces.

**billingGroupMaster toggle, root-caused (`docs/ANGULAR_PENDING_FINDINGS.md` entry 5):** two independent, stacked bugs, both confirmed empirically in a live browser against real dev data. (1) `new/src/styles.scss`'s `.slider` CSS has no `input:checked` state at all — the switch is visually a static gray bar in every state, confirmed by reading the stylesheet directly. (2) `toggleStatus()` mutates the signal-held array's row object in place rather than reloading via the service (unlike legacy's `initBillingGroupMaster()` full-reload pattern) — proven not to silently corrupt data (a forced unrelated re-render showed the mutation did land correctly) but fragile, since it only becomes visible when some *other* signal write happens to trigger a re-render. Confirmed the same CSS bug independently on `insuranceMaster` (after the DB-drift fix below unblocked real data testing there).

**Systemic finding, flagged as an immediate callout rather than held for the final report (`docs/ANGULAR_PENDING_FINDINGS.md` entry 6):** none of the 10 rebuilt routes (`new/src/app/app.routes.ts`, both pilot batches) carry any `canActivate` guard, while legacy enforces module/role-based access control via a `resolve` block repeated across at least 8 `.state()` definitions in `app.js`. Confirmed the severity precisely, per the owner's direct follow-up question: a raw `curl` with zero auth against the backend returns `401` on `organization/count`/`organization/1`/`organization/save` (so *some* valid login is required), but the same authenticated session that legacy's own UI explicitly denies access to `organizationMaster` (superuser-gated, confirmed via the real resolve condition `$cookies.getObject('cookieObject').isSuperUser!='Y'`) gets a clean `200` with real data through the rebuild's identical backend call — proving module/role permission is enforced *only* in legacy's frontend router, nowhere else, and the rebuild doesn't reproduce it. Net effect: any authenticated user of any role can reach any rebuilt screen's real data regardless of actual entitlement.

**Two local-dev-DB gaps found and closed, owner-approved mid-session (explicit override of this session's original "don't touch the backend" instruction, logged as `BR-local-dev-db-drift-insurance-misreports`):** (1) `adt.t_insurance` was missing `general_ledger_id`/`block_report_print` columns, 500ing on every list/save call — added both, additive-only, verified via a real create+toggle round-trip in-browser. (2) MISReports had zero report-group/report/parameter data and zero per-user report-access mapping — seeded one minimal synthetic report end-to-end (clearly named "Test Report"/"Test Report Group", not real data) spanning 5 representative parameter control types, discovering along the way that `m_reports_details.view_name` is a literal dynamic-SQL string Hibernate executes at runtime (not a display label as first assumed) and that per-user report visibility is gated by a `t_user_unit_mapper.mis_report_ids` JSON mapping most sessions won't have populated. This unblocked a real comparison that surfaced one further rebuild finding (entry 7: `unit`/`user`/`org` parameter controls are hard-disabled on the rebuild vs. real `<select>` elements on legacy — looks like a deliberate rebuild simplification per its own code comment, but not previously documented as a known deviation, and possibly a real regression if any actual report relies on cross-unit reporting).

**insuranceMaster and organizationMaster/inputOutputCharts sweep status:** insuranceMaster fully unblocked and tested (real save/toggle round-trip confirmed on both apps). `organizationMaster` remains untestable for functional parity with the only available test credential (correctly, deliberately superuser-gated in legacy — confirmed not a misconfiguration). `inputOutputCharts` remains blocked by a complete absence of patient/ward-assignment data in this dev DB; seeding that (a deeper, more interconnected clinical-data chain than the two fixes above) was raised with the owner but not undertaken this entry pending their decision on how far to go.

Sweep of batch 1's remaining screens (`MISReports` now unblocked, `inputOutputCharts` still blocked) and all of batch 2 (not yet started — needs its own git worktree + separate `ng serve` instance since it's an unmerged branch) continues in a following entry. No rule in `RULES.md` touched (all DB changes additive-only per RULES.md item 1; no local `try/catch` touched; no application code fixed based on any finding above, per standing instruction).

Role: Maker (Track A fix, held for review) + Blueprint (Track A2 investigation, Track B audits — findings only, nothing fixed).

**Track A — `fix/assigningpickerdispenser-storename-null-check` (held for review, not merged/pushed):** fixed the entry-9 crash (`assigningPickerDispenserController.js`, null-dereference on `sessionStorage.selectedTopStore` before its own guard's `if` branch even finishes) by moving the dereference into the guard's `else` branch, matching the sibling `OPassigningPickerDispenserController.js`'s check-before-dereference ordering. Verified two ways: (1) `tools/golden-trace/test_assigningpickerdispenser_storename_bug.js`, direct `$controller` instantiation with `sessionStorage` mocked null/set — re-ran against the original code first (`git stash`) to confirm the crash reproduces exactly as documented, then against the fix to confirm both states behave correctly; (2) real browser automation against the local dev Tomcat (`DEMO`/`Admin` session) — crash-state and normal-state click-throughs, each in isolation (a second, fresh tab was needed for the normal-state case after the first test's store-selection modal was found to persist across in-tab navigation, a Bootstrap-modal artifact of the test sequence, not a regression). On why the whole NABH cluster (SurgicalSiteInfection, HandHygieneSurvey, PatientFallReporting, UtiReporting, MedicationErrorReporting) was disabled together: no explicit comment/commit/doc found anywhere (this repo's history for these files is a single squashed baseline import). Reported as a plausible-but-unproven "deliberate, known-incomplete rollback" read, not a fact, per explicit instruction not to overstate.

Updated `docs/ANGULAR_PENDING_FINDINGS.md` (entry 11), `docs/ANGULAR_PHASE0_SCREEN_INVENTORY.md`, `docs/ANGULAR_REBUILD_PLAYBOOK.md`, and `docs/ANGULAR_PHASE4_SEQUENCING_RECOMMENDATION.md` to cite the corrected figure and logged the owner-approved first batch (14 low-risk, zero-cross-screen-coupling screens) in the sequencing doc.

Ran Step 0 (screen-is-real) + Step 1 (audit) on all 14 approved screens, via 5 parallel audit passes matching `docs/PHASE2_PILOT_AUDIT.md`'s exact rigor. **All 14 passed Step 0** — no dead routes or mockups in this batch, no slot-swap needed. Logged 17 new findings (`docs/ANGULAR_PENDING_FINDINGS.md` entries 16-32): two systemic SEVERITY 1 crash bugs recurring across 4-6 screens (entries 16-18 — the exact "guarded but not returned" variant of entry 9's bug class, for which Track A's fix this session is a directly-applicable pattern), and — most significantly — **two SEVERITY 1 findings on `bookView`** (entries 28-29), the most severe in this project's findings doc to date: the screen's entire patient context comes from a `sessionStorage` key written by 14 unrelated controllers with no patient ID in the URL, so a clinician navigating away and back (ordinary browser-back behavior) can silently see a different patient's complete clinical record with nothing on screen indicating a mismatch; separately, its History/Diagnosis tabs render plainly-editable fields with zero save path anywhere in the 4,011-line controller. `bookView` was tagged low-risk by Phase 0's automated classifier — flagged as a concrete instance of that classifier's own documented "provisional, not a clinical sign-off" caveat mattering in practice, worth the owner's attention before this screen (or the risk-tier assumption generally) is relied on further. Remaining findings (19-27, 30-32) are lower-severity: a broken OR-instead-of-AND search filter duplicated across 2 screens, a broken `$state.go()` fallback duplicated across 2 screens (reachable in one), several dead/unreachable UI elements, one stale-clone binding traced to a specific named source controller (same class as entries 6-7), and assorted copy-paste residue. **Nothing found during Track B was fixed** — audit/characterize only, per standing instruction, same discipline as every prior finding in this doc.

Held for owner review before any golden-trace/rebuild work begins on this batch, per explicit instruction (hard gate). No rule in `RULES.md` touched. `docs/BUSINESS_RULE_MANIFEST.md`: no new entry (no code changed on `main`; Track A's fix is characterization+fix-proposal-executed but held on its own branch, unmerged). | Track A: `legacy/HIS-WAR/src/main/webapp/resources/assets/js/controller/pharmacy/assigningPickerDispenserController.js`, `tools/golden-trace/test_assigningpickerdispenser_storename_bug.js` (branch `fix/assigningpickerdispenser-storename-null-check`, held, not pushed). **`docs/DECISION_NEEDED_BookViewStalePatientContext.md` and `docs/DECISION_NEEDED_BookViewUnsavableHistoryFields.md` written** for entries 28/29 (the two severity-1 bookView findings), each independently re-verified against source before finalizing (not just relayed from investigation) — both proposals *downgraded* their own finding's severity after deeper investigation: entry 28's real mechanism is a transient flash before an accidental (undocumented, fragile) ui-router redirect saves the page, not a sustained wrong-patient render; entry 29's "editable" fields turn out to never render at all (their backing arrays are never assigned in the controller — independently re-verified via grep), so the described data-loss scenario isn't currently reachable. Owner approved both as proposed.

**Implemented and verified, each on its own branch, held for review, not merged/pushed:**
- `fix/bookview-stale-patient-context` — `checkAppointment()` now validates `bookViewObj`'s `encounterId`/`patientId` against `sessionStorage` before trusting it. Verified via logic assertions, a `$controller`-instantiation test, and real browser automation reproducing the actual multi-screen bug scenario via the app's own `$rootScope.redirectBookViewUrl()` (synthetic in-memory data, not persisted) — confirmed the pre-fix leak of a wrong patient's name into scope, confirmed the fix stops it and produces a clearer error message than before ("Please select Patient.." vs. the prior "Something went wrong!!").
- `fix/asset-management-selectedtopstore-null-check` — Track A's exact fix pattern applied to `viewAssetScheduler`, `scheduleWorklist`, `assetScheduler`, `detailScheduleWorklist` after confirming each matches the identical root cause directly against source. Verified via unit test + real browser automation (2 of 4 screens reached via a direct `$state.go()` call through the real app's injector, since no seeded asset data exists in this dev environment to reach them via real navigation — consistent with the project's standing practice against fabricating dev data).
- `fix/cookieobjectappconfig-null-check` — a genuinely different root cause from Track A's pattern (no guard at all, vs. guard-that-falls-through), confirmed before implementing. Redirect target (`applicationConfiguration` vs. `login` — both patterns coexist across the app, 53 vs. 136 files respectively, no local sibling precedent) resolved by asking the owner directly rather than picking unilaterally; owner chose `applicationConfiguration`, matching the app's one designated shared helper (`$rootScope.checkCookies()`). Verified via unit test + real browser automation, including discovering and working around a stale browser-cache issue and an origin-wide (not tab-scoped) cookie-removal side effect.
- `fix/bookview-unsavable-history-fields` — `ng-if="false"` added to the 6 dead `ng-repeat` blocks (9 occurrences). While implementing the checkbox half of this fix, found the `DECISION_NEEDED` document's own claim was wrong: only 1 of the 4 "standalone checkboxes" (`noSignificantVaccination`) is actually live — the other 3 were already sitting inside pre-existing, unclosed HTML comments, confirmed by reading raw source. Applying the proposed fix to those 3 would have nested a new comment inside an already-open one, prematurely closing it and making dead markup live — a real bug this session would have introduced. Reverted those 3 edits, kept only the genuinely-needed one, and corrected the `DECISION_NEEDED` doc to disclose this rather than silently fixing the record. Verified via real browser automation across all affected History/Diagnosis/Vaccination sub-tabs — zero console errors, correct disabled-checkbox behavior confirmed via direct click test.

**2 further pre-existing bugs found and logged (not fixed) while verifying the `cookieObjectAppConfig` fix** (`docs/ANGULAR_PENDING_FINDINGS.md` entries 34-35): `applicationConfigurationController.js` — the designated fallback screen for a missing `cookieObjectAppConfig` — itself dereferences that same cookie with zero guard, affecting ~53 screens app-wide that already redirect there, not just the 2 fixed this session; and `pmScheduleController.js`'s unconditional `getAssetDetailsByCriteria()` call crashes whenever `init()` exits early for any reason (confirmed pre-existing via the file's own already-existing missing-`cookieObject` guard, unrelated to this session's fix) — confirmed via real browser testing that it doesn't block the redirect, just adds a silent console error.

Every fix's deployed Tomcat copy was temporarily updated for live testing, then restored to its exact original pre-fix state immediately afterward, each time. Per explicit instruction, Part 2 (golden-trace + rebuild of the 14-screen Phase 4 batch) was **not started** — held at its own gate pending review of Part 1's implementations. 2026-07-25 (later still) | Checker (self-correction) | Claude Code (Sonnet 5) | main | **Correcting a factual error in the immediately preceding entry.** That entry claimed `legacy/` is a separately git-initialized nested repository, distinct from the outer `HIS_Java_Angular_upgrade` repo, and that `git checkout` commands run from inside `legacy/` therefore don't affect the outer repo's branch or the `new/` working tree. This is **wrong**, confirmed by direct re-check: `find HIS_Java_Angular_upgrade -maxdepth 3 -name .git` finds exactly **one** `.git` directory, at the outer root; `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` run from inside `legacy/`, from inside `new/`, and from the outer root all return the same single path. There is only one repository. `legacy/` and `new/` are ordinary subdirectories of it, not nested repos. The actual, correct explanation for the earlier "branch switch reverted the Angular files" symptom (from a prior session) is the simpler, original one: `git checkout` run from *any* cwd inside this single repo switches the entire working tree, including `new/`'s files -- exactly as it would appear to do, with no special safety from running it inside `legacy/`. **Practical consequence, corrected:** switching branches from within `legacy/` is NOT safe with respect to `new/`'s working tree; the earlier session's habit of "confirm current branch before testing the rebuild in the browser" (established for the right reason, on a wrong theory) remains the correct practice going forward -- just for the right reason. No file other than this log entry changed as a result of this correction; no code, schema, or other doc touched. Flagging per this project's standing self-verification discipline (never let an unverified or incorrect technical claim stand once caught) rather than leaving the wrong theory in place. | docs/SESSION_LOG.md (this entry only) | N -- docs-only correction, governance-file exception, `RULES.md` itself not touched

2026-07-26 | Maker (session-instability investigation, batch 20/21 candidate verification) | Claude Code (Sonnet 5) | fix/session-token-validation-instability | While live-verifying batch 20/21 candidates, hit a severity-1, cross-cutting blocker (flagged to owner immediately, not batched): the authenticated session dies unpredictably, often within seconds of login, misreported as "Token Expired!" by `GenericService.js`'s generic 401 handler. Investigated live, root-cause not fully resolved despite substantial effort -- tested and ruled out, each with direct evidence: (1) literal 8-hour JWT expiry (decoded a real token's exp-iat, matched source exactly); (2) stale deployed bytecode (MD5 of deployed `JwtTokenUtil.class` matched a fresh compile); (3) cookie-size eviction (measured the request `Cookie` header collapsing from ~4950 to 4 bytes between consecutive requests -- real, fixed, but the fast failure still reproduced after the fix); (4) subscription-expiry handling (`/unit/subcription/check` confirmed returning a clean `200`/`success` via a live authenticated `$http` call from within the running app). Found and fixed two real, separate bugs along the way, neither the root cause of the fast pattern but genuine improvements: `cookieObjectAppConfig`/`cookieObjectAppConfigSecond` (~80 client-only UI config fields each) were being stored as real HTTP cookies round-tripping on every request -- added a `$cookies` service decorator redirecting storage for these two names to `sessionStorage`, transparently, with all 251+ existing read call sites left unmodified (measured: Cookie header ~4950 -> ~1430 bytes); `checkUserLogin()`'s 90-second housekeeping poll (archives this device's own already-closed-out prior login records, confirmed via `CHECK_OTHER_LOGIN`'s own macAddress/isLogin='N'/isLogout='Y'/status='C' query) unconditionally cleared the CURRENT session's cookie on every successful archive, an unrelated side effect -- removed. Used a temporary diagnostic (`System.out.println` in `JwtTokenFilter.java`, logging the raw Cookie/userId/unitId headers) to get direct server-side evidence rather than guessing further; removed before finalizing the branch, confirmed via diff. Root Tomcat restart cycle required twice (JDK 21 toolchain from the prior session's fix handled this cleanly) plus two `index.html` cache-bust version bumps (3.10->3.11->3.12) after discovering the browser was serving a stale cached `app.js` despite hard-reload attempts. The core fast-failure pattern remains unresolved and is fully documented, with every ruled-out hypothesis, in `docs/ANGULAR_PENDING_FINDINGS.md` under `PENDING-session-instability-intermittent-logout` -- not silently dropped. Owner explicitly decided to stop the deep-dive at this point (four hypotheses tested, diminishing returns) and proceed with batch 20/21 work accepting reduced live-session reliability as a known, disclosed limitation for this cycle. Branch held, not merged, awaiting owner approval.
